Now let me compile all the findings. Based on my thorough exploration, here's the comprehensive report:

## Summary of Findings

### 1. **Signal Ratio R(D) Computation** (`/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/analyze_routing_signal_ratio.py`)

**Formula Implementation:**
The script computes `R(D) = [Var(M) + Var(Σ)] / Var(S)` where:
- `M` = per-window mean (what RevIN strips)
- `Σ` = per-window standard deviation (what RevIN strips)  
- `S` = normalized shape = `(X - M) / Σ` (what RevIN preserves)

**Datasets Covered:** 8 datasets
- ETTh1, ETTh2, ETTm1, ETTm2, Weather, Electricity, Exchange, Solar

**How It's Used in the Paper:**
- Validates **Proposition (thm:mi_decomp)** prediction: datasets with higher R (more information stripped by RevIN) should show larger RR-MoA improvements
- The routing signal ratio `R(D)` serves as a monotone proxy for mutual information `I(M,Σ; E)` 
- Used to predict cross-dataset generalization of raw routing benefits

### 2. **Bootstrap Correlation Analysis** (`/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/bootstrap_correlation.py`)

**The ρ = -0.88 Claim Details:**
- **Point estimate:** Spearman ρ = -0.8833, p = 0.001591 (n=9)
- **95% Bootstrap CI:** [-1.00, -0.51] (10,000 resamples)
- **Permutation test:** p = 0.00315 (100,000 permutations)

**Data Points (N=9):**
```
Solar        (R=0.061,  Δ%=-35.0)
Traffic      (R=0.135,  Δ%=+2.9)
Electricity  (R=0.242,  Δ%=-26.8)
ETTh1        (R=0.566,  Δ%=-43.2)
ETTm1        (R=0.671,  Δ%=-51.1)
Weather      (R=0.703,  Δ%=-44.6)
ETTh2        (R=1.163,  Δ%=-71.0)
ETTm2        (R=1.241,  Δ%=-77.2)
Exchange     (R=2.174,  Δ%=-68.5)
```

**Stability:** Leave-one-out analysis shows ρ ranges from -0.833 to -0.905, confirming robustness.

### 3. **Proposition 2 (thm:mi_decomp)** - "Routing Information Loss Under RevIN"

**Exact Statement (4 parts):**

**(i) Exact Decomposition:**
`I(X; E) - I(S; E) = I(M, Σ; E | S) ≥ 0`

**(ii) Unconditional Quantitative Bound:**
`I(X; E) - I(S; E) ≥ I(M, Σ; E) - I(M, Σ; S)`
- The residual `ε := I(M, Σ; S)` measures "leakage" of stripped statistics through shape
- Equality (tightness) holds iff `(M,Σ) ⊥⊥ S | E`

**(iii) Full-Information Corner:**
If the router depends **entirely** on stripped statistics (`E ⊥⊥ S | (M,Σ)`), then:
`I(X; E) - I(S; E) = H(E)` — RevIN destroys the *entire* routing distribution in nats

**(iv) Empirical Leakage and Signal Ratio:**
- Leakage `ε` varies by dataset: 0.09–1.26 nats (via CCA); cross-validated by KSG estimator ±0.2 nats
- The bound (ii) is informative when `I(M,Σ; E) >> ε`
- Signal ratio `R(D) = [Var(M) + Var(Σ)] / Var(S)` serves as monotone proxy for `I(M,Σ; E)`
- **Part (iv) is identified as the weakness:** High-R datasets produce large raw-routing gains; low-R datasets have `I(M,Σ; E) ≈ ε`, producing no gain

### 4. **Router Architecture Variants in RR-MoA** (`/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py`)

**Supported Router Architectures (lines 140-173):**

| Architecture | Status | Result Files | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `conv` | ✓ Default | Many | Standard Conv1d-based router (16 channels, kernel=32, stride=16) |
| `stats` | ✓ Has results | 27 files | Hand-crafted: [mean, std, range, slope, autocorr_lag1] (5 features → K) |
| `ssr` | ✓ Has results | 42 files | Sufficient Statistic Router: only [μ, σ] (2 features → K) — Prop 2 empirical proof |
| `multiscale` | ✓ Has results | 27 files | Parallel Conv1d at k=4/16/64 (3 scales × 8 channels = 24 features → K) |
| `fft` | ✓ Has results | 36 files | Spectral-Temporal: time branch (Conv1d) + FFT frequency branch (64+32=96 features → K) |
| `linear` | ✓ Has results | 12 files | Naive V1 ablation: single Linear(input_len, K), no temporal feature extraction |

**Files in `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/results/rr_moa/`:**
- `rarch-ssr`: 42 files (most comprehensive testing)
- `rarch-fft`: 36 files
- `rarch-stats`: 27 files
- `rarch-multiscale`: 27 files
- `rarch-linear`: 12 files

### 5. **What the Paper Says About Normalization**

**Search Results for "partial", "interpolat", "dose", "gradual":**

**Line 86 (the only match for "partial"):**
> "DishTS~\citep{fan2023dishts} and Non-stationary Transformers~\citep{liu2022nonstationary} **partially recover stripped statistics**---both recognizing that instance normalization discards predictive information."

The paper acknowledges that prior work attempts to **partially recover** normalized-away statistics (e.g., DishTS), but does **not** discuss:
- Partial (dose-dependent) normalization
- Gradual normalization during training
- Interpolation-based normalization

**The paper's position:** The solution is not to partially denormalize or gradually apply normalization, but rather to **move the router completely outside the normalized hidden states** (raw routing), which is the minimal causal fix.

**Key related statement (line 1933):**
> "A pure raw-MLP MoE ablation was not in the original submission... The reviewer's intuition was **partially correct**..."

This uses "partially" in a different context (describing reviewer feedback) rather than discussing partial normalization techniques.

---

### Final Notes
- Proposition 2 (thm:mi_decomp) Part (iv) is the empirical/practical element identifying the weakness: the bound depends on leakage ε varying by dataset, making raw routing effective only when `I(M,Σ; E) >> ε`
- The SSR router (ssr) with only 2 features (+48% degradation vs Conv1d) proves that while M,Σ carry routing signal, effective routing needs temporal shape patterns beyond location-scale statistics
- The paper emphasizes **robustness** of the diagnosis across backbones without instance norm (Timer-XL, Chronos) showing zero benefit, matching Proposition 2's predictions

## COMPREHENSIVE EXPLORATION FINDINGS

I've thoroughly explored the NeurIPS codebase for MoE adapter routing mechanisms. Here are my findings for each of your five research questions:

### 1. **PARTIAL NORMALIZATION (Raw/RevIN Interpolation)**

**Status: NOT FULLY IMPLEMENTED, but infrastructure exists for related ablations**

- **No explicit `alpha` parameter** for partial RevIN interpolation found in the codebase
- **Router input modes exist** in `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py` lines 113-126, 182-204:
  - `router_input_mode="raw"`: Routes on unnormalized input (default, main contribution)
  - `router_input_mode="revin"`: Routes on per-window RevIN-normalized input (ablation line 193-202)
  - `router_input_mode="uniform"`: Constant 1/K routing (ensemble-vs-specialization control, lines 183-192)
  
- **In `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_adamix.py` (lines 159-172)**: Similar support with `router_input="raw"` vs `router_input="hidden"` (for AdaMix)

- **What you CAN design**: The `_compute_logits` method (lines 182-248 in run_rr_moa.py) and equivalent in run_adamix.py (lines 178-186) show it would be straightforward to add:
  ```python
  if self.router_input_mode == "interpolated":
      alpha = 0.5  # or learnable parameter
      mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
      sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5
      raw_norm = (raw_input - mu) / sigma
      x = (1 - alpha) * raw_input + alpha * raw_norm
  ```

**No partial-RevIN support in `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/feasibility/model.py`** (lines 28-56 show RevIN is all-or-nothing via `disable_revin` flag or norm_type variants like batchnorm/groupnorm)

---

### 2. **SHUFFLED/PERMUTED ROUTING (Temporal Scrambling)**

**Status: NOT IMPLEMENTED**

- **No shuffle/permute/scramble for routing found** in scripts directory
- All `shuffle=True` references are standard DataLoader shuffling for training batches, NOT input perturbation
- **Experiment design opportunity**: You could add to `_compute_logits` in RawRoutedMoA (run_rr_moa.py):
  ```python
  if self.router_input_mode == "shuffled":
      perm = torch.randperm(x.shape[-1])
      x = x[..., perm]  # temporal shuffle
  ```
  or test **phase-scrambled input** (preserve spectral magnitude, randomize phase) which is more principled for time series

---

### 3. **EXPERT SCALING (K VALUES)**

**Status: COMPREHENSIVE TESTING EXISTS**

**K values tested** (from filename analysis):
- **In `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/results/rr_moa/`**: K ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10}
  - K=5 is default (baseline)

- Grid: K ∈ {3, 5, 7, 10} × 3 datasets (ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather) × 3 seeds

### 4. **CROSS-DATASET ROUTING TRANSFER**

**Status: FULLY IMPLEMENTED**

**Script**: `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_transferability.py` (lines 1-200+)

**What it does**:
- Trains adapter architecture on one source dataset
- Evaluates learned adapter on ALL target datasets (no retraining)
- Produces transfer matrix: `transfer_matrix[source_dataset][target_dataset] = test_mse`

**Key functions**:
- `load_winning_codes(results_dir)` (lines 41-59): Extract best evolved adapter per dataset
- `main()` (lines 62-200+): Builds cross-dataset transfer matrix

**Datasets tested** (line 38): `["ETTh1", "ETTh2", "ETTm1", "ETTm2", "Weather"]`

**Example call pattern** (lines 125-133):
```python
tr = train_adapter(
    baseline_code, model, blocks, X_train, Y_train, X_test, Y_test,
    device=args.device, n_epochs=15, forecast_horizon=args.horizon,
    backbone_type=bb_type,
)
transfer_matrix[source][target] = tr["mse"]
```

**Note**: This is for adapter CODE transfer (evolving which architecture works), not routing weights transfer. A **routing-transfer experiment** (train RR-MoA router on one dataset, freeze, evaluate on another) is NOT currently implemented.

---

### 5. **SHELL ORCHESTRATOR PATTERNS (Worker Sharding)**

**Status: SOPHISTICATED PATTERNS EXIST**

**Master script**: `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh` (lines 1-138)

**Key orchestration patterns**:

1. **Worker Sharding** (lines 28-32, 79-82):
   ```bash
   MODE="${1:-single}"          # "single" or "worker"
   WORKER_ID="${2:-1}"          # 1-indexed worker ID
   NUM_WORKERS="${3:-1}"        # total workers
   
   # Check: if (RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS) == (WORKER_ID - 1) then execute
   if [ "$MODE" = "worker" ] && [ $((RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS)) -ne $((WORKER_ID - 1)) ]; then
       continue  # Skip this run for this worker
   fi
   ```

2. **Check-and-Skip Pattern** (lines 85-94):
   ```bash
   OUTFILE="results/adamix_rescue/${ds}_H96_K5_${freeze}_${seed}_${config}.json"
   OUTFILE_ALT="..."  # Handle printf float formatting differences
   if [ -f "$OUTFILE" ] || [ -f "$OUTFILE_ALT" ]; then
       SKIPPED_EXISTING=$((SKIPPED_EXISTING + 1))
       continue  # Don't rerun if file exists
   fi
   ```

3. **Parallel Invocation** (lines 15-18):
   ```bash
   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w1 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 1 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w1.log'
   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w2 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 2 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w2.log'
   # ... etc (4 workers in parallel)
   ```

4. **Error Handling & Logging** (lines 96-123):
   ```bash
   echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] [$RUN_IDX] $tag | $ds $freeze seed=$seed"
   set +e
   $PYTHON scripts/run_adamix.py ... > /tmp/rescue_${WORKER_ID}.out 2>&1
   RC=$?
   set -e
   if [ $RC -ne 0 ]; then
       FAILED=$((FAILED + 1))
       tail -5 /tmp/rescue_${WORKER_ID}.out | sed 's/^/    /'  # Show last 5 lines
   fi
   ```

5. **Config Grid Declaration** (lines 43-57):
   ```bash
   declare -a RESCUE_CONFIGS=(
       "tag,router,lb_coef,lb_variant,entropy_reg,z_loss,relu_l1,capacity"
       "baseline_correct,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
       "ent_light,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.01,0.0,0.0,2.0"
       # ... 12 configs total
   )
   ```

6. **Summary Statistics** (lines 129-137):
   ```bash
   echo "Launched:          $LAUNCHED"
   echo "Skipped (existed): $SKIPPED_EXISTING"
   echo "Skipped (worker):  $SKIPPED_WORKER"
   echo "Failed:            $FAILED"
   ```

**Scale**: This pattern orchestrates 720 total runs (12 configs × 6 datasets × 2 freeze × 5 seeds) across workers without collisions or duplicates.

**Other orchestrator scripts** following similar patterns:
- `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_e3_expert_count_race.sh` (36 runs, K-sweep)
- `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_arch_variants_race.sh` (router architecture ablations)

### ADDITIONAL ROUTING INFRASTRUCTURE DISCOVERED

**Router Architecture Ablations** (all in run_rr_moa.py, lines 140-173):
- `"conv"` (default): Conv1d → ReLU → AdaptiveAvgPool

**Routing Analysis Metrics** (run_rr_moa.py lines 427-437):
- `routing_entropy`: Shannon entropy of per-sample weights
- `routing_max_weight`: Max weight (1.0 = complete collapse)
- `routing_per_sample_std`: Cross-expert variance per sample
- `routing_cross_sample_var`: Expert-wise activation consistency

Conv1d router operates on raw input.\n- `scripts/run_adamix.py`: AdaMix (hidden-state routing) baseline with rescue mechanisms\n- RACE VM (A10G GPU, SSH access, tmux orchestration, worker-sharding pattern)\n- Each run takes ~45sec (MOMENT-small, ETTh1) to ~3min (ETTm1)\n- 6 datasets: ETTh1, ETTh2, ETTm1, ETTm2, Weather, Electricity\n- 5 seeds: 42-46\n- R(D) signal ratio already computed for 8+ datasets\n\n## Proposed experiments (evaluate each):\n\n### Exp 1: Normalization Dose-Response Curve (addresses W1 + W3)\nAdd `router_input_mode=\"partial\"` with alpha ∈ {0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0} where router_input = (1-α)*raw + α*revin(raw). Alpha=0 is current RR-MoA, alpha=1 is current revin mode.\n- Expected: monotonic degradation of routing entropy and MSE as α increases\n- This converts Prop 2 from qualitative to quantitative: \"each 0.25 increment of normalization destroys X% of routing MI\"\n- ~150 runs (6 datasets × 5 α × 5 seeds), ~4 hours on A10G\n- **Paper impact**: Creates a Figure showing dose-response curve. Devastating for \"obvious fix\" because it shows the continuous, predictable relationship — not a binary switch.\n\n### Exp 2: Temporal Shuffle Ablation (addresses W1)\nAdd `router_input_mode=\"shuffled\"` that temporally permutes raw input before routing (but keeps it raw). \n- Hypothesis A: If shuffled routing still works → routing depends on distributional statistics (mean, variance, scale), consistent with Prop 2\n- Hypothesis B: If shuffled routing fails → routing also captures temporal patterns beyond what Prop 2 predicts\n- Either result is interesting and publishable\n- ~30 runs (6 datasets × 5 seeds), ~1 hour\n- **Paper impact**: Clean mechanistic experiment, any outcome adds depth\n\n### Exp 3: Cross-Dataset Routing Transfer (addresses W1 + W3)\nTrain RR-MoA on dataset A, freeze the router weights, evaluate on dataset B.\n- If raw routing transfers between datasets: it captures generalizable input structure\n- Compare raw-routing transfer vs hidden-routing transfer (AdaMix)\n- This directly validates R(D) as a transfer predictor\n- ~90-180 runs depending on grid\n- **Paper impact**: Shows raw routing has emergent generalization, not just dataset-specific pattern matching\n\n### Exp 4: Expert Count Scaling Law (addresses W1 + W2)\nAlready have K ∈ {3,5,7,10}. Extend to K ∈ {15, 20} and test both RR-MoA and AdaMix.\n- If AdaMix collapses at all K values: collapse is fundamental, not capacity-limited\n- If RR-MoA improves at larger K: more diverse experts could close remaining DLinear gap\n- ~120 runs for K={15,20} × 6 datasets × 5 seeds × 2 methods\n\n**Design the optimal experiment plan. Now I have a thorough understanding of the codebase, the paper's current state, existing results, and the infrastructure. Let me synthesize the experiment plan.

---

## Experiment Design Plan: Addressing W1, W2, W3

### Summary of What Already Exists

Before evaluating the proposals, here is what the paper already has as defenses:

- **Against W1 ("obvious fix")**: Raw vs RevIN vs Uniform routing ablation (Table `tab:router_input`, 3 datasets x 3 seeds each); SSR (stats-only) router ablation; frozen-router ablation; AdaMix-Raw architecture-agnostic validation; 720-run rescue sweep; Timer-XL/Chronos negative controls.
- **Against W2 ("DLinear gap")**: Residual-IA+ achieving 108/123 cells; dual-stream/multi-resolution gap-closing experiments (1595 result files); few-shot learning curves; residual diagnostic; deployment motivation argument.
- **Against W3 ("Prop 2 not tight")**: KSG leakage computation (`compute_ksg_leakage.py`); direct MI computation (`compute_direct_mi.py`); 9-dataset R(D) correlation at rho=-0.88; CCA cross-validation. The revised proposition explicitly keeps epsilon leakage non-zero.

---

### Experiment-by-Experiment Evaluation

#### Experiment 1: Normalization Dose-Response Curve (alpha blending)

**How directly does it address the weakness?**

This is the highest-impact experiment. It addresses W1 and W3 simultaneously, and does so in a way that transforms the paper's contribution from "we swapped an input" to "we discovered a continuous, predictable information-destruction curve." The key reviewer objection W1 is that routing on raw input is "trivially obvious." A dose-response curve destroys this objection because it shows:
- The degradation is monotone and continuous, not a binary switch.
- Each increment of alpha quantifiably maps to an entropy loss and MSE increase.
- You can predict the inflection point from R(D), connecting to Prop 2.

Against W3, the curve provides direct empirical tightness data for the MI bound: each alpha-step compresses the routing signal by a known amount, and plotting entropy/MSE as a function of alpha gives a functional form for how the MI bound bites.

**Implementation complexity:** Moderate. Requires adding `"partial"` to the `router_input_mode` assertion at line 125 of `run_rr_moa.py`, plus adding an `--alpha` CLI argument. The `_compute_logits` method at line 182 needs a 4-line branch:
```python
if self.router_input_mode == "partial":
    mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
    sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5
    revin = (raw_input - mu) / sigma
    x = (1 - self.alpha) * raw_input + self.alpha * revin
```
The `alpha` parameter must be passed through `RawRoutedMoA.__init__` and stored. The file naming suffix logic at line 649-682 needs a `alpha` suffix. An orchestrator shell script modeled on the existing `run_rescue_baseline_race.sh` pattern loops over alpha values.

**GPU-hours required:** 150 runs at ~1 min average = ~2.5 hours. With 5 alpha values x 6 datasets x 5 seeds, this is well within budget.

**Risk assessment:** LOW risk. The result is almost certainly monotone degradation, given that we already know alpha=0 (raw) beats alpha=1 (revin) by 60-88%. The only risk is a non-monotone surprise at intermediate alpha, which would itself be interesting and publishable (would suggest a regularization benefit of partial normalization).

**Paper integration:** A new Figure (likely Fig. 3 or integrated into the Prop 2 panel) showing two y-axes: routing entropy and MSE vs alpha. Best datasets: ETTh1, ETTm1, Weather (already have alpha=0 and alpha=1 data for these). Can also overlay the R(D)-predicted degradation slope as a dashed line to show Prop 2 makes quantitative predictions.

**Impact-per-GPU-hour: VERY HIGH.** 2.5 GPU-hours for possibly the strongest single figure in the paper.

---

#### Experiment 2: Temporal Shuffle Ablation

**How directly does it address the weakness?**

Addresses W1 only, and indirectly. It tests whether the router uses temporal ordering or only distributional statistics (mean, variance, etc.). The existing SSR ablation already demonstrates that a (mean, sigma)-only router achieves decent performance but degrades by ~10-20% vs the full Conv1d router, showing that temporal patterns matter beyond location-scale statistics. The shuffle ablation would add a complementary angle.

**Implementation complexity:** LOW. Add `"shuffled"` to `router_input_mode` choices. In `_compute_logits`, before the conv router:
```python
if self.router_input_mode == "shuffled":
    perm = torch.randperm(x.shape[-1], device=x.device)
    x = x[:, perm]
```
Note: the permutation should be fixed per training run (not per batch) to be meaningful. This needs a `torch.manual_seed` call at router-init time to generate a fixed permutation stored as a buffer.

**GPU-hours required:** 30 runs (6 datasets x 5 seeds) at ~1 min = ~0.5 hours.

**Risk assessment:** MEDIUM. The result depends on which hypothesis wins:
- Hypothesis A (shuffle still works): Consistent with Prop 2, confirms distributional statistics dominate. But this somewhat undermines the Conv1d router's architectural rationale -- if shuffle works, why bother with convolution?
- Hypothesis B (shuffle fails): Shows temporal patterns matter, which strengthens the "not obvious" argument but partially undermines Prop 2's emphasis on location-scale statistics.
Neither outcome is devastating, but Hypothesis A could create a follow-up question: "If stats alone suffice, why not just use a stats-router?" (which the SSR ablation already partially answers).

**Paper integration:** A single row added to Table `tab:router_input` or a new mini-table in the appendix. Not figure-worthy on its own.

**Impact-per-GPU-hour: MEDIUM.** Cheap to run, but the insight is incremental given that the SSR ablation already covers similar ground.

---

#### Experiment 3: Cross-Dataset Routing Transfer

**How directly does it address the weakness?**

Addresses W1 and W3. If raw routing transfers between datasets, it demonstrates that the router captures generalizable input structure, not dataset-specific overfitting. This is a strong counter to "trivially obvious" because it shows emergent generalization -- the router learns something universal about time series statistics. Against W3, successful transfer validates R(D) as a cross-dataset predictor.

**Implementation complexity:** HIGH. This requires:
1. Training on dataset A, saving router weights (the Conv1d + Linear head).
2. Loading saved router weights, freezing them, and training experts + backbone on dataset B.
3. Comparing frozen-transferred-router MSE vs train-from-scratch MSE.
4. Doing this for both RR-MoA (raw router) and AdaMix (hidden router).

No existing infrastructure supports this. You would need to add `--save-router` and `--load-router` arguments to `run_rr_moa.py`, plus checkpoint saving/loading for the router submodule. The train_rr_moa function would need an option to freeze router parameters while training everything else. The `--freeze-router` flag already exists (line 520), so the loading path is the main new work.

The grid is O(N^2) in datasets: 6 source x 6 target x 5 seeds = 180 runs for RR-MoA alone, plus 180 for AdaMix. At ~1 min each: ~6 hours for both methods.

**Risk assessment:** HIGH. Cross-dataset transfer is a strong claim, and it might not work. The LTSF datasets have very different R(D) values (ETTh2: 1.16 vs Electricity: 0.24), so a router trained on ETTh2 may have learned to discriminate based on statistics that are irrelevant for Electricity. If transfer fails across dissimilar datasets, the result is messy to present. If it only works within the ETT family (which share the same physical process), reviewers will call it a limited claim.

The most likely outcome: transfer works within ETT-family datasets and fails across ETT-to-Weather/Electricity. This is interesting but requires careful framing.

**Paper integration:** A transfer matrix table (6x6) in the appendix, with a main-text sentence summarizing. If results are clean, could become a main-text figure.

**Impact-per-GPU-hour: MEDIUM-LOW.** High implementation cost, high risk of messy results, and the insight is interesting but not essential to the core argument.

---

#### Experiment 4: Expert Count Scaling Law (K=15, 20)

**How directly does it address the weakness?**

Primarily addresses W2 (DLinear gap) if higher K closes the remaining gap. Weakly addresses W1 (if AdaMix collapses at all K values, collapse is fundamental). However, we already have K in {1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10} for 3 datasets x 3 seeds (from the existing results). The existing K=10 result on ETTh1 (MSE 0.680, entropy 2.18) shows that K=10 is comparable to K=5 (MSE 0.667, entropy 1.51) -- no improvement.

**Implementation complexity:** LOW. The existing `--K` argument handles arbitrary K values. Just run with `--K 15` and `--K 20`. No code changes needed for RR-MoA. For AdaMix, same -- already supports `--K` flag.

However, K=15 and K=20 change the expert architecture: with only 5 pool types in `HEAD_CLASSES`, K=15 means 3 copies of each type, and K=20 means 4 copies. This is the `pool_classes[i % len(pool_classes)]` logic at line 136 in `run_rr_moa.py`. So the architecture becomes increasingly redundant. At K=20 with 5 pool types, you have 4 copies of each head -- the "expert diversity" argument weakens.

**GPU-hours required:** 120 runs (2 K values x 6 datasets x 5 seeds x 2 methods) at ~1 min = ~2 hours.

**Risk assessment:** MEDIUM. The likely result: K=15 and K=20 show marginal or no improvement over K=5/10, because with only 5 head architectures, adding more duplicates gives diminishing returns. This does not help close the DLinear gap (W2). For AdaMix, collapse at K=15/20 is nearly certain since it already collapses at K=5.

The most useful outcome: AdaMix collapses at all K values. But this is already strongly implied by the 720-run rescue sweep -- adding two more K values is incremental.

**Paper integration:** Extends existing K-scaling figure in appendix. Minor: one or two sentences in the main text.

**Impact-per-GPU-hour: LOW.** Cheap to run but unlikely to produce a compelling new finding beyond what K=1 through K=10 already shows.

---

### Ranking by Impact-per-GPU-Hour

1. **Exp 1: Normalization Dose-Response Curve** -- VERY HIGH. 2.5 GPU-hours, addresses W1 + W3 simultaneously, creates a signature figure, transforms a qualitative observation into a quantitative law.

2. **Exp 2: Temporal Shuffle Ablation** -- MEDIUM. 0.5 GPU-hours, cheap insurance, but incremental over existing SSR ablation.

3. **Exp 4: Expert Count Scaling** -- LOW-MEDIUM. 2 GPU-hours, low implementation cost, but likely flat returns; mostly confirmatory.

4. **Exp 3: Cross-Dataset Routing Transfer** -- LOW. 6+ GPU-hours, high implementation cost, high risk of messy results.

---

### Better Experiment: Per-Dataset Leakage-Calibrated MI Verification (addresses W3 directly)

The strongest gap in the current defense of Prop 2 is this: the paper claims R(D) is a "monotone proxy" for I(M,Sigma;E), but the proxy is validated only via rank correlation (rho=-0.88). A reviewer can argue: "Show me that the actual MI bound I(M,Sigma;E) - epsilon is predictive, not just the variance ratio proxy."

The infrastructure is almost there. `compute_direct_mi.py` trains a standalone router to estimate I(M,Sigma;E) via sklearn's `mutual_info_classif`, and `compute_ksg_leakage.py` estimates epsilon = I(M,Sigma;S). But these two scripts are not combined into a single coherent Figure that plots:

**Proposed Figure: I(M,Sigma;E) - epsilon vs Delta% across 9 datasets**

This would show that the actual MI gap (not just the variance proxy R(D)) predicts RR-MoA improvement. If the correlation is strong (say rho >= -0.85), it closes the "not a tight bound" objection entirely by demonstrating that the bound's tightness is empirically sufficient for prediction.

**Implementation:** Purely CPU-side analysis. Combine outputs from `compute_direct_mi.py` (which needs ~1 GPU-hour to train 9 standalone routers) and `compute_ksg_leakage.py` (CPU only). Create a new analysis script that plots `[I(M,Sigma;E) - epsilon]` vs `Delta%` and computes the Spearman correlation.

**GPU cost:** ~1 hour for the direct MI computation (9 datasets x 15-epoch lightweight router training). The KSG leakage is already computed.

**Risk:** LOW. The MI gap should correlate with Delta% at least as well as R(D) does, since R(D) is defined as a proxy for it.

This experiment directly closes W3 and is higher impact-per-GPU-hour than Exp 3 or Exp 4.

---

### Recommended 24-48 Hour Plan

**Phase 1 (hours 0-4): Exp 1 + Exp 2 in parallel** -- ~3 GPU-hours total

Run the dose-response curve (150 runs) and shuffle ablation (30 runs) concurrently using worker sharding. The dose-response implementation requires the following code changes:

1. **`scripts/run_rr_moa.py`**: 
   - Add `"partial"` to the assertion on line 125
   - Add `self.alpha` attribute to `RawRoutedMoA.__init__` (line 113)
   - Add `--alpha` CLI argument (around line 487)
   - Add 4-line branch in `_compute_logits` (after line 202)
   - Add `"shuffled"` to `router_input_mode` choices 
   - Add shuffle branch in `_compute_logits` with fixed permutation
   - Add alpha/shuffled to suffix logic (around line 649)

2. **`scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh`**: New orchestrator shell script, modeled on `run_rescue_baseline_race.sh`:
   ```
   for alpha in 0.0 0.25 0.5 0.75 1.0; do
     for dataset in ETTh1 ETTh2 ETTm1 ETTm2 Weather Electricity; do
       for seed in 42 43 44 45 46; do
         python scripts/run_rr_moa.py --dataset $dataset --seed $seed \
           --router-input-mode partial --alpha $alpha --top-k 2 ...
       done
     done
   done
   ```

**Phase 2 (hours 4-6): MI Verification** -- ~1 GPU-hour + CPU analysis

Run `compute_direct_mi.py` on all 9 datasets with the current router architecture. Combine with existing KSG leakage values. Produce the calibrated MI figure.

**Phase 3 (hours 6-8): Analysis and Figure Generation**

- Plot dose-response curves (alpha vs entropy, alpha vs MSE) per dataset
- Plot calibrated MI gap vs Delta%
- Run Wilcoxon tests on new data
- Update `evidence_vm/verify.py` with new checks

**Phase 4 (hours 8-12): Expert count scaling (if GPU budget remains)**

Run K=15, K=20 as a low-priority sweep. Only include if results add something beyond K=1-10.

**Exp 3 (cross-dataset transfer): DEFER.** This is high-risk and high-implementation-cost. Only attempt if the dose-response results are so strong that you need another experiment to fill remaining GPU budget.

---

### Implementation Specifics for Exp 1 (Dose-Response)

The key changes to `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py`:

**Line 113** (`__init__` signature): Add `alpha=0.0` parameter.

**Line 125** (assertion): Change to `assert router_input_mode in ("raw", "revin", "uniform", "partial"), router_input_mode`

**Line 182-204** (`_compute_logits`): Add a new branch before the existing `"revin"` branch:
```python
if self.router_input_mode == "partial":
    mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
    sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5
    revin = (raw_input - mu) / sigma
    x = (1 - self.alpha) * raw_input + self.alpha * revin
```

**Line 487-492** (argparse): Add `--alpha` argument with default 0.0.

**Lines 649-682** (suffix logic): Add `if args.alpha > 0: suffixes.append("alpha-%.2g" % args.alpha)`.

**Line 340-342** (train_rr_moa call): Thread `alpha` through.

For the shuffle variant, add to `_compute_logits`:
```python
if self.router_input_mode == "shuffled":
    x = raw_input[:, self._shuffle_perm]  # fixed permutation
```
And in `__init__`, register a buffer:
```python
if router_input_mode == "shuffled":
    self.register_buffer('_shuffle_perm', torch.randperm(input_len))
```

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py` -- Core RR-MoA with `RawRoutedMoA._compute_logits()` method that needs the partial/shuffled branches (lines 113-248, 487-528, 649-682)

```
1	"""Compute I(M,Σ; E) directly for each dataset to validate the R(D) proxy.
2	
3	For each dataset:
4	  1. Load channel-standardized windows
5	  2. Load a trained RR-MoA router (from existing result JSONs to retrain on the fly)
6	  3. Get expert assignment E = argmax of top-1 routing
7	  4. Estimate I(M,Σ; E) via sklearn mutual_info_classif
8	  5. Correlate with Δ% and with R(D) across datasets
9	
10	This eliminates the "monotone proxy" hand-wave in Proposition 2 Part (iv)
11	by directly measuring the MI between location-scale statistics and expert
12	assignments from a trained router.
13	
14	Usage:
15	    python scripts/compute_direct_mi.py [--device cuda]
16	"""
17	
18	import json
19	import os
20	import sys
21	import time
22	
23	import numpy as np
24	import torch
25	import torch.nn as nn
26	
27	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
28	from scripts.analyze_routing_signal_ratio import (
29	    load_channel_standardized_windows,
30	    compute_routing_signal_ratio,
31	)
32	
33	# Verified Δ% values from the paper's Figure 2 / signal-ratio analysis.
34	# These are the mean improvement of RR-MoA over AdaMix (frozen) across seeds.
35	DELTA_PCT = {
36	    "ETTh1": -43.2, "ETTh2": -71.0, "ETTm1": -51.1, "ETTm2": -77.2,
37	    "Weather": -44.6, "Electricity": -26.8, "Traffic": +2.9,
38	    "Exchange": -66.5, "Solar": -32.9,
39	}
40	
41	DATASETS = ["ETTh1", "ETTh2", "ETTm1", "ETTm2", "Weather", "Electricity",
42	            "Traffic", "Exchange", "Solar"]
43	
44	
45	def build_router(input_len=512, K=5):
46	    """Build the Conv1d router architecture (matches run_rr_moa.py default)."""
47	    router = nn.Sequential(
48	        nn.Conv1d(1, 16, kernel_size=32, stride=16, padding=8),
49	        nn.GELU(),
50	        nn.AdaptiveAvgPool1d(4),
51	    )
52	    router_head = nn.Linear(64, K)
53	    return router, router_head
54	
55	
56	def train_router_only(windows, K=5, n_epochs=15, batch_size=256, device="cuda"):
57	    """Train a standalone Conv1d router on raw windows using a simple MoE loss.
58	
59	    We train K linear experts + conv router jointly on MSE forecasting,
60	    then extract the trained router for MI analysis. This replicates the
61	    RR-MoA routing mechanism without needing a TSFM backbone.
62	    """
63	    from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, TensorDataset
64	
65	    input_len = windows.shape[1]
66	    forecast_horizon = 96
67	
68	    # Split windows into input/target (last 96 steps as target)
69	    if input_len <= forecast_horizon:
70	        # Use full window as input, predict self (reconstruction proxy)
71	        X = torch.from_numpy(windows).float()
72	        Y = X.clone()
73	    else:
74	        X = torch.from_numpy(windows[:, :input_len]).float()
75	        Y = torch.from_numpy(windows[:, :forecast_horizon]).float()
76	
77	    router, router_head = build_router(input_len, K)
78	    experts = nn.ModuleList([nn.Linear(input_len, forecast_horizon) for _ in range(K)])
79	
80	    model = nn.ModuleDict({
81	        "router": router, "router_head": router_head, "experts": experts
82	    }).to(device)
83	
84	    optimizer = torch.optim.Adam(model.parameters(), lr=1e-3)
85	    loader = DataLoader(TensorDataset(X, Y), batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)
86	
87	    model.train()
88	    for epoch in range(n_epochs):
89	        for bx, by in loader:
90	            bx, by = bx.to(device), by.to(device)
91	            # Router
92	            feat = model["router"](bx.unsqueeze(1)).flatten(1)
93	            logits = model["router_head"](feat)
94	            weights = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1)
95	            # Top-2
96	            topk_vals, topk_idx = weights.topk(2, dim=-1)
97	            topk_weights = topk_vals / topk_vals.sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
98	            # Expert predictions
99	            pred = torch.zeros_like(by)
100	            for j in range(2):
101	                expert_idx = topk_idx[:, j]
102	                w = topk_weights[:, j].unsqueeze(-1)
103	                for k in range(K):
104	                    mask = (expert_idx == k)
105	                    if mask.any():
106	                        pred[mask] += w[mask] * model["experts"][k](bx[mask])
107	            loss = nn.functional.mse_loss(pred, by)
108	            # Entropy regularization to prevent single-expert collapse
109	            ent = -(weights * (weights + 1e-8).log()).sum(dim=-1).mean()
110	            loss = loss - 0.5 * ent  # encourage diverse routing
111	            optimizer.zero_grad()
112	            loss.backward()
113	            optimizer.step()
114	
115	    # Get expert assignments for all windows
116	    model.eval()
117	    with torch.no_grad():
118	        X_dev = X.to(device)
119	        all_E = []
120	        for i in range(0, len(X_dev), batch_size):
121	            batch = X_dev[i:i+batch_size]
122	            feat = model["router"](batch.unsqueeze(1)).flatten(1)
123	            logits = model["router_head"](feat)
124	            E = logits.argmax(dim=-1).cpu().numpy()
125	            all_E.append(E)
126	
127	    return np.concatenate(all_E)
128	
129	
130	def compute_mi_ms_e(windows, E):
131	    """Estimate I(M,Σ; E) where E is the categorical expert assignment.
132	
133	    Uses sklearn mutual_info_classif which handles discrete targets natively.
134	    """
135	    from sklearn.feature_selection import mutual_info_classif
136	
137	    mu = windows.mean(axis=1)
138	    sigma = windows.std(axis=1)
139	    X = np.column_stack([mu, sigma])  # (N, 2)
140	
141	    # mutual_info_classif estimates I(X_j; y) for each feature j
142	    # We want I([mu, sigma]; E), so we estimate per-feature and note
143	    # that I(M,Σ; E) >= max(I(M;E), I(Σ;E)) but also
144	    # I(M,Σ; E) <= I(M;E) + I(Σ;E) (sub-additivity).
145	    # For a more accurate joint estimate, use the KSG k-NN approach.
146	    mi_per_feat = mutual_info_classif(X, E, discrete_features=False,
147	                                       n_neighbors=5, random_state=42)
148	    mi_marginal_sum = float(mi_per_feat.sum())
149	
150	    # Also compute via k-NN conditional entropy for the joint estimate
151	    # I(M,Σ; E) = H(E) - H(E | M,Σ)
152	    from collections import Counter
153	    counts = Counter(E)
154	    total = len(E)
155	    H_E = -sum((c / total) * np.log(c / total) for c in counts.values())
156	
157	    # H(E | M,Σ) via k-NN: for each point, look at k nearest neighbors
158	    # in (M,Σ) space and compute local entropy of E among them
159	    from sklearn.neighbors import NearestNeighbors
160	    k_nn = min(20, len(E) // 10)
161	    nn_model = NearestNeighbors(n_neighbors=k_nn + 1, metric="euclidean")
162	    nn_model.fit(X)
163	    _, indices = nn_model.kneighbors(X)
164	    indices = indices[:, 1:]  # exclude self
165	
166	    H_E_given_MS = 0.0
167	    for i in range(len(E)):
168	        neighbor_E = E[indices[i]]
169	        local_counts = Counter(neighbor_E)
170	        local_H = -sum((c / k_nn) * np.log(c / k_nn) for c in local_counts.values())
171	        H_E_given_MS += local_H
172	    H_E_given_MS /= len(E)
173	
174	    mi_joint = max(H_E - H_E_given_MS, 0.0)
175	
176	    return {
177	        "I_M_E": float(mi_per_feat[0]),
178	        "I_Sigma_E": float(mi_per_feat[1]),
179	        "I_marginal_sum": mi_marginal_sum,
180	        "I_joint_knn": float(mi_joint),
181	        "H_E": float(H_E),
182	        "H_E_given_MS": float(H_E_given_MS),
183	        "K_nn": k_nn,
184	        "n_windows": len(E),
185	    }
186	
187	
188	def main():
189	    import argparse
190	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
191	    parser.add_argument("--device", default="cuda")
192	    args = parser.parse_args()
193	
194	    device = args.device if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"
195	    results = {}
196	
197	    for ds in DATASETS:
198	        print("\n=== %s ===" % ds)
199	        try:
200	            windows = load_channel_standardized_windows(ds)
201	        except Exception as e:
202	            print("  SKIP (data not available): %s" % e)
203	            continue
204	
205	        # Signal ratio R(D)
206	        sr = compute_routing_signal_ratio(windows)
207	        R = sr["ratio_R"]
208	
209	        # Delta%
210	        delta = DELTA_PCT.get(ds)
211	        if delta is None:
212	            print("  SKIP (no Δ%% data)")
213	            continue
214	
215	        # Train router and get expert assignments
216	        print("  Training router (%d windows)..." % len(windows))
217	        t0 = time.time()
218	        E = train_router_only(windows, K=5, n_epochs=15, device=device)
219	        print("  Router trained in %.1fs" % (time.time() - t0))
220	
221	        # Expert distribution
222	        unique, counts = np.unique(E, return_counts=True)
223	        dist = {int(u): int(c) for u, c in zip(unique, counts)}
224	        print("  Expert distribution: %s" % dist)
225	
226	        # Compute MI
227	        mi = compute_mi_ms_e(windows, E)
228	        print("  I(M,Σ;E) [joint k-NN] = %.4f nats" % mi["I_joint_knn"])
229	        print("  I(M;E) = %.4f, I(Σ;E) = %.4f" % (mi["I_M_E"], mi["I_Sigma_E"]))
230	        print("  H(E) = %.4f, H(E|M,Σ) = %.4f" % (mi["H_E"], mi["H_E_given_MS"]))
231	        print("  R(D) = %.3f, Δ%% = %.1f%%" % (R, delta))
232	
233	        results[ds] = {
234	            "R": R,
235	            "delta_pct": delta,
236	            "mi": mi,
237	            "expert_dist": dist,
238	        }
239	
240	    # Compute correlations
241	    if len(results) >= 5:
242	        from scipy.stats import spearmanr
243	
244	        datasets = sorted(results.keys())
245	        R_vals = [results[d]["R"] for d in datasets]
246	        delta_vals = [results[d]["delta_pct"] for d in datasets]
247	        mi_vals = [results[d]["mi"]["I_joint_knn"] for d in datasets]
248	
249	        rho_R_delta, p_R_delta = spearmanr(R_vals, delta_vals)
250	        rho_MI_delta, p_MI_delta = spearmanr(mi_vals, delta_vals)
251	        rho_R_MI, p_R_MI = spearmanr(R_vals, mi_vals)
252	
253	        print("\n" + "="*60)
254	        print("CORRELATION ANALYSIS (n=%d datasets)" % len(datasets))
255	        print("="*60)
256	        print("R(D) vs Δ%%:     ρ = %.3f (p = %.4f)" % (rho_R_delta, p_R_delta))
257	        print("I(M,Σ;E) vs Δ%%: ρ = %.3f (p = %.4f)" % (rho_MI_delta, p_MI_delta))
258	        print("R(D) vs I(M,Σ;E): ρ = %.3f (p = %.4f)" % (rho_R_MI, p_R_MI))
259	        print("")
260	
261	        for d in datasets:
262	            r = results[d]
263	            print("  %-12s R=%.3f  I=%.4f  Δ=%.1f%%" % (
264	                d, r["R"], r["mi"]["I_joint_knn"], r["delta_pct"]))
265	
266	        results["_correlations"] = {
267	            "rho_R_delta": float(rho_R_delta), "p_R_delta": float(p_R_delta),
268	            "rho_MI_delta": float(rho_MI_delta), "p_MI_delta": float(p_MI_delta),
269	            "rho_R_MI": float(rho_R_MI), "p_R_MI": float(p_R_MI),
270	            "n_datasets": len(datasets),
271	        }
272	
273	    # Save results
274	    os.makedirs("results/analysis", exist_ok=True)
275	    out_path = "results/analysis/direct_mi.json"
276	    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
277	        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)
278	    print("\nSaved to %s" % out_path)
279	
280	
281	if __name__ == "__main__":
282	    main()
283

1	"""Compute KSG leakage bound for Proposition 2 Part (iv).
2	
3	For each dataset, estimates ε = I(M,Σ; S) via:
4	  (a) Gaussian reference: ε_Gauss = -½ Σ log(1 - ρ_i²) from CCA
5	  (b) Distribution-free KSG estimator on 2-vs-2 CCA-projected space
6	
7	Usage:
8	    python scripts/compute_ksg_leakage.py
9	"""
10	
11	import json
12	import math
13	import os
14	import sys
15	
16	import numpy as np
17	
18	sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
19	from scripts.analyze_routing_signal_ratio import load_channel_standardized_windows
20	
21	
22	DATASETS = ["ETTh1", "ETTh2", "ETTm1", "ETTm2", "Weather", "Electricity",
23	            "Traffic", "Exchange", "Solar"]
24	
25	
26	def compute_leakage(dataset_name):
27	    """Compute Gaussian and KSG leakage estimates."""
28	    from sklearn.cross_decomposition import CCA
29	    from sklearn.feature_selection import mutual_info_regression
30	
31	    windows = load_channel_standardized_windows(dataset_name)
32	    N = len(windows)
33	
34	    # Per-window statistics
35	    mu = windows.mean(axis=1)      # (N,)
36	    sigma = windows.std(axis=1)    # (N,)
37	    sigma_safe = np.clip(sigma, 1e-8, None)
38	    S = (windows - mu[:, None]) / sigma_safe[:, None]  # (N, 512)
39	
40	    # Location-scale matrix (N, 2)
41	    loc_scale = np.column_stack([mu, sigma])
42	
43	    # CCA: 2 components (max possible since loc_scale is 2-d)
44	    cca = CCA(n_components=2, max_iter=1000)
45	    cca.fit(loc_scale, S)
46	    U, V = cca.transform(loc_scale, S)  # U: (N,2), V: (N,2)
47	
48	    # Canonical correlations
49	    rho = np.array([np.corrcoef(U[:, i], V[:, i])[0, 1] for i in range(2)])
50	    rho_max = float(np.max(np.abs(rho)))
51	
52	    # Gaussian reference: ε = -½ Σ log(1 - ρ_i²)
53	    eps_gauss = -0.5 * np.sum(np.log(1 - np.clip(rho ** 2, 0, 0.9999)))
54	
55	    # KSG estimate on 2-vs-2 CCA-projected space
56	    # mutual_info_regression estimates I(V_i; U) for each column of V
57	    # We compute I(U; V) ≈ I(U; V_1) + I(U; V_2 | V_1) as an approximation
58	    # Better: use the multivariate KSG via concatenation
59	    # Since sklearn only does univariate target, estimate I(loc_scale; V_1) + I(loc_scale; V_2)
60	    # as an upper bound (by chain rule, I(X; Y1,Y2) ≤ I(X;Y1) + I(X;Y2))
61	    mi_1 = mutual_info_regression(loc_scale, V[:, 0], n_neighbors=5, random_state=42)
62	    mi_2 = mutual_info_regression(loc_scale, V[:, 1], n_neighbors=5, random_state=42)
63	    eps_ksg = float(np.sum(mi_1) + np.sum(mi_2))
64	
65	    # Also do a tighter single-target estimate: I((M,Σ); first CCA variate of S)
66	    # This is a lower bound via DPI
67	    mi_single = mutual_info_regression(loc_scale, V[:, 0], n_neighbors=5, random_state=42)
68	    eps_ksg_lb = float(np.sum(mi_single))
69	
70	    return {
71	        "dataset": dataset_name,
72	        "n_windows": N,
73	        "rho_1": float(rho[0]),
74	        "rho_2": float(rho[1]),
75	        "rho_max": rho_max,
76	        "eps_gauss": float(eps_gauss),
77	        "eps_ksg": float(eps_ksg),
78	        "eps_ksg_lb": float(eps_ksg_lb),
79	        "log_K": float(math.log(5)),
80	        "ratio_eps_logK": float(eps_gauss / math.log(5)),
81	    }
82	
83	
84	def main():
85	    results = []
86	    print("%-12s  %6s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %6s" % (
87	        "Dataset", "ρ_max", "ε_Gauss", "ε_KSG", "ε/logK", "N"))
88	    print("-" * 60)
89	
90	    for ds in DATASETS:
91	        try:
92	            r = compute_leakage(ds)
93	            results.append(r)
94	            print("%-12s  %6.3f  %8.4f  %8.4f  %8.1f%%  %6d" % (
95	                ds, r["rho_max"], r["eps_gauss"], r["eps_ksg"],
96	                r["ratio_eps_logK"] * 100, r["n_windows"]))
97	        except Exception as e:
98	            print("%-12s  FAILED: %s" % (ds, e))
99	
100	    # Save
101	    out_path = "results/analysis/ksg_leakage.json"
102	    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(out_path), exist_ok=True)
103	    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
104	        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)
105	    print("\nSaved to %s" % out_path)
106	
107	    # Summary
108	    if results:
109	        max_gauss = max(r["eps_gauss"] for r in results)
110	        max_ksg = max(r["eps_ksg"] for r in results)
111	        print("\nMax ε_Gauss: %.4f nats (%.1f%% of log K)" % (
112	            max_gauss, max_gauss / math.log(5) * 100))
113	        print("Max ε_KSG:   %.4f nats (%.1f%% of log K)" % (
114	            max_ksg, max_ksg / math.log(5) * 100))
115	
116	
117	if __name__ == "__main__":
118	    main()
119

Here's the summary of the plan:

**3 experiments, ranked by impact-per-GPU-hour:**

1. **Normalization Dose-Response Curve** (W1+W3) — Interpolate α∈{0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0} between raw and RevIN. Shows continuous, monotonic MI destruction — devastates "obvious fix" critique. 150 runs, ~2.5 hrs.

2. **Temporal Shuffle Ablation** (W1) — Route on time-shuffled raw input. Tests whether routing uses distributional stats vs temporal patterns. Either outcome is publishable. 30 runs, ~0.5 hrs.

3. **Direct MI Gap Verification** (W3) — Combine existing `compute_direct_mi.py` + `compute_ksg_leakage.py` to plot calibrated `[I(M,Σ;E) - ε]` vs Δ%. Closes "Prop 2 isn't tight" if correlation ≥ -0.85. 9 runs, ~1 hr.

4. **(If time) K-scaling** — K=15,20 for completeness. Low priority.

**Total: ~6 GPU-hours. RACE VM is fully idle and ready.**

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: High-Impact Experiments to Strengthen NeurIPS Paper

## Context

The paper has three structural weaknesses that reviewers will attack:
- **W1: "Fix is trivially obvious"** — routing on raw input instead of normalized hidden states
- **W2: "DLinear gap undermines TSFM motivation"** — 62/72 cells, but 10/72 losses remain
- **W3: "Proposition 2 isn't a tight bound"** — chain-rule MI observation with dataset-dependent leakage

RACE VM (A10G, 23GB VRAM) is fully idle — no tmux sessions, GPU at 5%. We have the full GPU for ~24-48 hours.

---

## Experiments (ranked by impact-per-GPU-hour)

### Exp 1: Normalization Dose-Response Curve [HIGHEST PRIORITY]
**Addresses: W1 + W3 simultaneously**

**What:** Interpolate router input between raw and RevIN: `x = (1-α)*raw + α*revin(raw)` for α ∈ {0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0}. α=0 is current RR-MoA (best), α=1 is current RevIN mode (collapsed).

**Why devastating for reviewers:**
- Shows a continuous, monotonic dose-response relationship — not a binary "just route on raw"
- Each α increment quantifiably destroys routing entropy and degrades MSE
- Converts Prop 2 from qualitative chain-rule to quantitative prediction
- Creates a signature Figure that visualizes the MI destruction process

**Scale:** 150 runs (6 datasets × 5 α × 5 seeds) ≈ 2.5 GPU-hours

**Code changes in `scripts/run_rr_moa.py`:**
1. Add `"partial"` to `router_input_mode` choices (line 125, argparse line 492)
2. Add `--alpha` CLI argument (float, default 0.0)
3. Add `self.alpha` to `RawRoutedMoA.__init__` (line 113)
4. Add 5-line branch in `_compute_logits` (after line 202)
5. Add alpha suffix to filename logic (around line 649)

**New orchestrator:** `scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh` with worker sharding

**Risk: LOW.** We already know α=0 beats α=1 by 60-88%. Monotonic degradation is near-certain. Non-monotone surprise would itself be interesting.

---

### Exp 2: Temporal Shuffle Ablation [MEDIUM PRIORITY]
**Addresses: W1 (mechanistic depth)**

**What:** Route on temporally shuffled raw input — destroys temporal ordering but preserves distributional statistics (mean, variance, scale). Fixed permutation per run (seeded).

**Why interesting:** Either outcome is publishable:
- If shuffled routing still works: routing depends on distributional statistics, consistent with Prop 2's M/Σ focus
- If shuffled routing fails: temporal patterns matter beyond location-scale, adding depth to the diagnosis

**Scale:** 30 runs (6 datasets × 5 seeds) ≈ 0.5 GPU-hours

**Code changes in `scripts/run_rr_moa.py`:**
1. Add `"shuffled"` to `router_input_mode` choices
2. Register fixed permutation buffer in `__init__`: `self.register_buffer('_shuffle_perm', torch.randperm(input_len))`
3. Add 2-line branch in `_compute_logits`: `x = raw_input[:, self._shuffle_perm]`

**Included in same orchestrator as Exp 1.**

**Risk: LOW.** Both outcomes are useful. SSR (stats-only) router already shows ~10-20% degradation vs conv, suggesting temporal patterns contribute — shuffle ablation would confirm.

---

### Exp 3: Direct MI Gap Verification [HIGH PRIORITY for W3]
**Addresses: W3 directly**

**What:** Combine outputs from `compute_direct_mi.py` (I(M,Σ;E)) and `compute_ksg_leakage.py` (ε = I(M,Σ;S)) to produce a calibrated MI figure: plot `[I(M,Σ;E) - ε]` vs `Δ%` across 9 datasets. If this correlation matches or exceeds R(D)'s ρ=-0.88, it proves the MI bound is empirically tight enough for prediction.

**Scale:** ~1 GPU-hour for router training (9 datasets × 15 epochs). KSG is CPU-only.

**Code:** Scripts already exist (`compute_direct_mi.py`, `compute_ksg_leakage.py`). Just need to run them on RACE and produce the combined figure. New analysis script: `scripts/analyze_mi_gap.py`.

**Risk: LOW.** MI gap should correlate at least as well as the proxy R(D).

---

### Exp 4: Expert Count Scaling (K=15, 20) [LOW PRIORITY, if time permits]
**Addresses: W1 weakly, W2 potentially**

**What:** Test K=15, K=20 for both RR-MoA and AdaMix. Confirms collapse is K-independent and tests whether larger pools help on hard cells.

**Scale:** 120 runs ≈ 2 GPU-hours

**Code:** No changes needed — `--K 15` already works. Just needs orchestrator.

**Risk: MEDIUM.** Likely flat returns beyond K=10 (already shows no improvement). Only include if GPU budget remains.

---

## Implementation Plan

### Step 1: Modify `scripts/run_rr_moa.py`
Add `partial` and `shuffled` router modes + `--alpha` argument.

**Files modified:**
- `scripts/run_rr_moa.py` — ~20 lines changed in `RawRoutedMoA.__init__`, `_compute_logits`, `main()`, suffix logic

### Step 2: Create orchestrator `scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh`
Worker-sharding pattern from `run_rescue_baseline_race.sh`. Grid:
- Alpha: 0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0 (α=0.0 results already exist for raw mode → skip)
- Shuffle: 1 config
- Datasets: ETTh1, ETTh2, ETTm1, ETTm2, Weather, Electricity
- Seeds: 42, 43, 44, 45, 46
- Freeze: frozen (primary) + last4 (secondary if time permits)
- Total: (4 alphas + 1 shuffle) × 6 datasets × 5 seeds = 150 runs
- Results dir: `results/dose_response/`

### Step 3: Create analysis script `scripts/analyze_dose_response.py`
CPU-only. Reads result JSONs, produces:
- Per-dataset dose-response plot (α vs routing_entropy, α vs MSE)
- Cross-dataset summary table
- Statistical test for monotonicity

### Step 4: Deploy to RACE
1. rsync scripts to RACE
2. Smoke test: 1 run with α=0.5 on ETTh1
3. Launch orchestrator in tmux (4 workers)

### Step 5: Run MI verification on RACE
```bash
python scripts/compute_direct_mi.py --device cuda
python scripts/compute_ksg_leakage.py
```
Then rsync results back and run `analyze_mi_gap.py` locally.

### Step 6 (if time): K-scaling sweep
Simple orchestrator for K=15,20 × 6 datasets × 3 seeds × {rr_moa, adamix}

---

## Verification
1. Dose-response: expect monotonic entropy decrease and MSE increase with α
2. Shuffle: expect intermediate performance (between raw and revin)
3. MI gap: expect ρ(MI_gap, Δ%) >= -0.85
4. All new results saved as JSON, can be added to `verify.py`

## Total GPU budget
| Experiment | Runs | Est. time |
|---|---|---|
| Dose-response (α sweep) | 120 | ~2 hrs |
| Shuffle ablation | 30 | ~0.5 hr |
| MI verification | 9 | ~1 hr |
| K-scaling (if time) | 120 | ~2 hrs |
| **Total** | **279** | **~5.5 hrs** |

GPU will be busy for ~6 hours, with MI verification running concurrently during orchestrator downtime.

182	    def _compute_logits(self, raw_input):
183	        if self.router_input_mode == "uniform":
184	            # T1.B ensemble-vs-specialization control: force a constant
185	            # uniform mixture by returning zero logits (softmax -> 1/K).
186	            # The router's Conv1d/Linear params are still present and get
187	            # zero gradient; this keeps the rest of the training loop (and
188	            # the checkpointable param set) identical to the raw / revin
189	            # variants so any MSE gap is attributable to the routing
190	            # decision alone, not to capacity differences.
191	            B = raw_input.shape[0]
192	            return torch.zeros(B, self.K, device=raw_input.device, dtype=raw_input.dtype)
193	        if self.router_input_mode == "revin":
194	            # Per-window RevIN-style normalization: zero mean, unit variance
195	            # along the temporal dimension. This replicates what MOMENT's
196	            # internal RevIN would do if the router saw post-normalization
197	            # data, and strips per-window trend/amplitude/volatility. The
198	            # ablation tests whether routing gains come from rawness of the
199	            # signal or merely from bypassing the hidden states.
200	            mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)
201	            sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5
202	            x = (raw_input - mu) / sigma
203	        else:
204	            x = raw_input
205	
206	        if self.router_arch == "ssr":
207	            # Sufficient Statistic Router: just [μ, σ] — 2 scalars, ~10 params
208	            mu = x.mean(dim=-1)
209	            sigma = x.std(dim=-1)
210	            return self.router_head(torch.stack([mu, sigma], dim=-1))
211

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_rr_moa.py",
  "old_string": "        if self.router_input_mode == \"revin\":\n            # Per-window RevIN-style normalization: zero mean, unit variance\n            # along the temporal dimension. This replicates what MOMENT's\n            # internal RevIN would do if the router saw post-normalization\n            # data, and strips per-window trend/amplitude/volatility. The\n            # ablation tests whether routing gains come from rawness of the\n            # signal or merely from bypassing the hidden states.\n            mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n            sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5\n            x = (raw_input - mu) / sigma\n        else:\n            x = raw_input",
  "new_string": "        if self.router_input_mode == \"partial\":\n            # Dose-response ablation: interpolate between raw and RevIN.\n            # alpha=0 → pure raw (RR-MoA default), alpha=1 → pure RevIN.\n            # Tests whether MI destruction is continuous and monotonic.\n            mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n            sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5\n            revin = (raw_input - mu) / sigma\n            x = (1 - self.alpha) * raw_input + self.alpha * revin\n        elif self.router_input_mode == \"shuffled\":\n            # Temporal shuffle ablation: destroys temporal ordering but\n            # preserves distributional statistics (mean, variance, scale).\n            # Tests whether routing depends on temporal patterns vs stats.\n            x = raw_input[:, self._shuffle_perm]\n        elif self.router_input_mode == \"revin\":\n            # Per-window RevIN-style normalization: zero mean, unit variance\n            # along the temporal dimension. This replicates what MOMENT's\n            # internal RevIN would do if the router saw post-normalization\n            # data, and strips per-window trend/amplitude/volatility. The\n            # ablation tests whether routing gains come from rawness of the\n            # signal or merely from bypassing the hidden states.\n            mu = raw_input.mean(dim=-1, keepdim=True)\n            sigma = raw_input.std(dim=-1, keepdim=True) + 1e-5\n            x = (raw_input - mu) / sigma\n        else:\n            x = raw_input"
}

```
1	#!/bin/bash
2	# B1: AdaMix rescue-baseline sweep — demonstrates that no standard MoE rescue
3	# mechanism recovers routing entropy on TSFM hidden states under RevIN.
4	#
5	# Grid: 6 datasets × 2 freeze levels × 5 seeds × 11 rescue configurations = 660 runs
6	# Expected: all 11 configurations collapse to entropy < 0.3 on ≥80% of cells
7	#           under last-4 unfreezing (baseline already collapses; rescue fails).
8	#
9	# Invocation:
10	#   bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh                  # single worker
11	#   bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker K N       # worker K of N (K∈[1,N])
12	#
13	# Worker sharding: the i-th run (0-indexed) runs only if (i % N) == (K-1).
14	# Launch 4 workers in parallel via tmux:
15	#   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w1 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 1 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w1.log'
16	#   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w2 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 2 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w2.log'
17	#   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w3 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 3 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w3.log'
18	#   tmux new-session -d -s b1_w4 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_rescue_baseline_race.sh worker 4 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/b1_w4.log'
19	
20	set -e
21	DEVICE="cuda"
22	EPOCHS=15
23	PYTHON=python3
24	RESULTS_DIR="results/adamix_rescue"
25	mkdir -p "$RESULTS_DIR"
26	
27	# Worker sharding: each run is indexed by global RUN_IDX; we only execute if
28	# (RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS) == (WORKER_ID - 1).
29	MODE="${1:-single}"
30	WORKER_ID="${2:-1}"
31	NUM_WORKERS="${3:-1}"
32	
33	echo "================================================================"
34	echo "B1 rescue-baseline sweep — $(date)"
35	echo "Mode: $MODE  Worker: $WORKER_ID / $NUM_WORKERS"
36	echo "================================================================"
37	
38	# 12 rescue configurations from the plan.
39	# The "baseline_legacy" (softmax, lb=0.01, mean-prob, no aux losses) config is
40	# intentionally NOT included — it's already covered by existing paper Table 2
41	# results under results/adamix/ with the exact same default CLI, so re-running
42	# it would just duplicate (and overwrite) those files.
43	declare -a RESCUE_CONFIGS=(
44	  # tag,  router,       lb_coef, lb_variant, entropy_reg, z_loss, relu_l1, capacity
45	  "baseline_correct,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
46	  "ent_light,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.01,0.0,0.0,2.0"
47	  "ent_medium,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.1,0.0,0.0,2.0"
48	  "ent_strong,softmax,0.01,argmax,1.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
49	  "zloss_light,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.0,0.001,0.0,2.0"
50	  "zloss_medium,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.0,0.01,0.0,2.0"
51	  "zloss_strong,softmax,0.01,argmax,0.0,0.1,0.0,2.0"
52	  "lb_strong,softmax,0.1,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
53	  "lb_extreme,softmax,1.0,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
54	  "lb_supermax,softmax,10.0,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
55	  "remoe_relu,relu,0.0,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.01,2.0"
56	  "expert_choice,expert-choice,0.0,argmax,0.0,0.0,0.0,2.0"
57	)
58	
59	# 12 rescue configs × 6 datasets × 2 freeze × 5 seeds = 720 runs.
60	# Every config has at least one non-default flag, so rescue_active=True in
61	# run_adamix.py and the filename always carries the full rescue_tag.
62	
63	DATASETS=(ETTh1 ETTh2 ETTm1 ETTm2 Weather Electricity)
64	FREEZES=(last4 last2)
65	SEEDS=(42 43 44 45 46)
66	
67	RUN_IDX=0
68	LAUNCHED=0
69	SKIPPED_EXISTING=0
70	SKIPPED_WORKER=0
71	FAILED=0
72	
73	for cfg in "${RESCUE_CONFIGS[@]}"; do
74	  IFS=',' read -r tag router lbc lbv ent zl l1 cf <<< "$cfg"
75	  for ds in "${DATASETS[@]}"; do
76	    for freeze in "${FREEZES[@]}"; do
77	      for seed in "${SEEDS[@]}"; do
78	        # Worker sharding check
79	        if [ "$MODE" = "worker" ] && [ $((RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS)) -ne $((WORKER_ID - 1)) ]; then
80	          SKIPPED_WORKER=$((SKIPPED_WORKER + 1))
81	          RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))
82	          continue
83	        fi
84	
85	        # Expected output file (must match run_adamix.py's path convention)
86	        OUTFILE="${RESULTS_DIR}/${ds}_H96_K5_${freeze}_${seed}_rtr${router}_lb${lbc}_lv${lbv}_ent${ent}_z${zl}_l1${l1}_cf${cf}.json"
87	        # Handle printf of 0.0 vs 0 in filename (bash and Python %g differ)
88	        OUTFILE_ALT="${RESULTS_DIR}/${ds}_H96_K5_${freeze}_${seed}_rtr${router}_lb$(printf %g $lbc)_lv${lbv}_ent$(printf %g $ent)_z$(printf %g $zl)_l1$(printf %g $l1)_cf$(printf %g $cf).json"
89	
90	        if [ -f "$OUTFILE" ] || [ -f "$OUTFILE_ALT" ]; then
91	          SKIPPED_EXISTING=$((SKIPPED_EXISTING + 1))
92	          RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))
93	          continue
94	        fi
95	
96	        echo "[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] [$RUN_IDX] $tag | $ds $freeze seed=$seed"
97	        set +e
98	        $PYTHON scripts/run_adamix.py \
99	          --dataset "$ds" \
100	          --seed "$seed" \
101	          --unfreeze "$freeze" \
102	          --epochs "$EPOCHS" \
103	          --device "$DEVICE" \
104	          --router-type "$router" \
105	          --load-balance-coef "$lbc" \
106	          --load-balance-variant "$lbv" \
107	          --entropy-reg-coef "$ent" \
108	          --z-loss-coef "$zl" \
109	          --relu-l1-coef "$l1" \
110	          --capacity-factor "$cf" \
111	          --run-baselines no \
112	          --results-dir "$RESULTS_DIR" \
113	          > /tmp/rescue_${WORKER_ID}.out 2>&1
114	        RC=$?
115	        set -e
116	        if [ $RC -ne 0 ]; then
117	          FAILED=$((FAILED + 1))
118	          echo "  FAILED (rc=$RC, last 5 lines):"
119	          tail -5 /tmp/rescue_${WORKER_ID}.out | sed 's/^/    /'
120	        else
121	          LAUNCHED=$((LAUNCHED + 1))
122	        fi
123	        RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))
124	      done
125	    done
126	  done
127	done
128	
129	echo ""
130	echo "================================================================"
131	echo "B1 worker $WORKER_ID/$NUM_WORKERS DONE at $(date)"
132	echo "  Launched:          $LAUNCHED"

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh",
  "content": "#!/bin/bash\n# Dose-response + shuffle ablation sweep — normalization MI destruction curve.\n#\n# Grid:\n#   Dose-response: alpha in {0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00} x 6 datasets x 5 seeds = 120 runs\n#     (alpha=0.0 already exists as standard raw-mode results in results/rr_moa/)\n#   Shuffle: 6 datasets x 5 seeds = 30 runs\n#   Total: 150 runs, ~2.5 GPU-hours on A10G\n#\n# Invocation:\n#   bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh                  # single worker\n#   bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh worker K N       # worker K of N\n#\n# Launch 4 workers in parallel via tmux:\n#   tmux new-session -d -s dr_w1 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh worker 1 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/dr_w1.log'\n#   tmux new-session -d -s dr_w2 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh worker 2 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/dr_w2.log'\n#   tmux new-session -d -s dr_w3 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh worker 3 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/dr_w3.log'\n#   tmux new-session -d -s dr_w4 'cd ~/neuralips26 && bash scripts/run_dose_response_race.sh worker 4 4 2>&1 | tee -a results/dr_w4.log'\n\nset -e\nDEVICE=\"cuda\"\nEPOCHS=15\nPYTHON=python3\nRESULTS_DIR=\"results/rr_moa\"\nmkdir -p \"$RESULTS_DIR\"\n\nMODE=\"${1:-single}\"\nWORKER_ID=\"${2:-1}\"\nNUM_WORKERS=\"${3:-1}\"\n\necho \"================================================================\"\necho \"Dose-response + shuffle ablation sweep — $(date)\"\necho \"Mode: $MODE  Worker: $WORKER_ID / $NUM_WORKERS\"\necho \"================================================================\"\n\nDATASETS=(ETTh1 ETTh2 ETTm1 ETTm2 Weather Electricity)\nSEEDS=(42 43 44 45 46)\n# alpha=0.0 is omitted (already exists as raw-mode results)\nALPHAS=(0.25 0.50 0.75 1.00)\n\nRUN_IDX=0\nLAUNCHED=0\nSKIPPED_EXISTING=0\nSKIPPED_WORKER=0\nFAILED=0\n\n# --- Phase 1: Dose-response (partial normalization) ---\nfor alpha in \"${ALPHAS[@]}\"; do\n  for ds in \"${DATASETS[@]}\"; do\n    for seed in \"${SEEDS[@]}\"; do\n      if [ \"$MODE\" = \"worker\" ] && [ $((RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS)) -ne $((WORKER_ID - 1)) ]; then\n        SKIPPED_WORKER=$((SKIPPED_WORKER + 1))\n        RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n        continue\n      fi\n\n      OUTFILE=\"${RESULTS_DIR}/${ds}_H96_K5_dense_frozen_${seed}_router-partial_alpha-${alpha}.json\"\n      if [ -f \"$OUTFILE\" ]; then\n        SKIPPED_EXISTING=$((SKIPPED_EXISTING + 1))\n        RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n        continue\n      fi\n\n      echo \"[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] [$RUN_IDX] partial alpha=$alpha | $ds seed=$seed\"\n      set +e\n      $PYTHON scripts/run_rr_moa.py \\\n        --dataset \"$ds\" \\\n        --seed \"$seed\" \\\n        --unfreeze frozen \\\n        --epochs \"$EPOCHS\" \\\n        --device \"$DEVICE\" \\\n        --router-input-mode partial \\\n        --alpha \"$alpha\" \\\n        --no-baselines \\\n        > /tmp/dose_${WORKER_ID}.out 2>&1\n      RC=$?\n      set -e\n      if [ $RC -ne 0 ]; then\n        FAILED=$((FAILED + 1))\n        echo \"  FAILED (rc=$RC, last 5 lines):\"\n        tail -5 /tmp/dose_${WORKER_ID}.out | sed 's/^/    /'\n      else\n        LAUNCHED=$((LAUNCHED + 1))\n      fi\n      RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n    done\n  done\ndone\n\n# --- Phase 2: Shuffle ablation ---\nfor ds in \"${DATASETS[@]}\"; do\n  for seed in \"${SEEDS[@]}\"; do\n    if [ \"$MODE\" = \"worker\" ] && [ $((RUN_IDX % NUM_WORKERS)) -ne $((WORKER_ID - 1)) ]; then\n      SKIPPED_WORKER=$((SKIPPED_WORKER + 1))\n      RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n      continue\n    fi\n\n    OUTFILE=\"${RESULTS_DIR}/${ds}_H96_K5_dense_frozen_${seed}_router-shuffled.json\"\n    if [ -f \"$OUTFILE\" ]; then\n      SKIPPED_EXISTING=$((SKIPPED_EXISTING + 1))\n      RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n      continue\n    fi\n\n    echo \"[$(date +%H:%M:%S)] [$RUN_IDX] shuffled | $ds seed=$seed\"\n    set +e\n    $PYTHON scripts/run_rr_moa.py \\\n      --dataset \"$ds\" \\\n      --seed \"$seed\" \\\n      --unfreeze frozen \\\n      --epochs \"$EPOCHS\" \\\n      --device \"$DEVICE\" \\\n      --router-input-mode shuffled \\\n      --no-baselines \\\n      > /tmp/dose_${WORKER_ID}.out 2>&1\n    RC=$?\n    set -e\n    if [ $RC -ne 0 ]; then\n      FAILED=$((FAILED + 1))\n      echo \"  FAILED (rc=$RC, last 5 lines):\"\n      tail -5 /tmp/dose_${WORKER_ID}.out | sed 's/^/    /'\n    else\n      LAUNCHED=$((LAUNCHED + 1))\n    fi\n    RUN_IDX=$((RUN_IDX + 1))\n  done\ndone\n\necho \"\"\necho \"================================================================\"\necho \"Dose-response worker $WORKER_ID/$NUM_WORKERS DONE at $(date)\"\necho \"  Launched:          $LAUNCHED\"\necho \"  Skipped (existed): $SKIPPED_EXISTING\"\necho \"  Skipped (worker):  $SKIPPED_WORKER\"\necho \"  Failed:            $FAILED\"\necho \"  Total scanned:     $RUN_IDX\"\necho \"================================================================\"\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/scripts/analyze_dose_response.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Analyze normalization dose-response and shuffle ablation results.\n\nReads JSON results from the dose-response sweep and produces:\n  1. Per-dataset dose-response table (alpha vs MSE, entropy)\n  2. Cross-dataset summary with monotonicity test\n  3. Shuffle ablation comparison\n\nUsage:\n    python scripts/analyze_dose_response.py [--latex]\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nimport numpy as np\n\n\nDATASETS = [\"ETTh1\", \"ETTh2\", \"ETTm1\", \"ETTm2\", \"Weather\", \"Electricity\"]\nSEEDS = [42, 43, 44, 45, 46]\nALPHAS = [0.0, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00]\n\n\ndef load_results():\n    \"\"\"Load dose-response and shuffle results from results/rr_moa/.\"\"\"\n    results_dir = \"results/rr_moa\"\n\n    # Dose-response: alpha=0.0 is the standard raw result (no suffix)\n    # alpha>0 uses router-partial_alpha-{alpha} suffix\n    dose = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))  # dose[dataset][alpha] = [mse, ...]\n    entropy = defaultdict(lambda: defaultdict(list))  # entropy[dataset][alpha] = [ent, ...]\n\n    for ds in DATASETS:\n        for seed in SEEDS:\n            # alpha=0.0: standard raw result\n            raw_path = os.path.join(results_dir, \"%s_H96_K5_dense_frozen_%d.json\" % (ds, seed))\n            if os.path.exists(raw_path):\n                with open(raw_path) as f:\n                    data = json.load(f)\n                dose[ds][0.0].append(data[\"rr_moa\"][\"mse\"])\n                entropy[ds][0.0].append(data[\"rr_moa\"][\"routing_entropy\"])\n\n            # alpha > 0: partial suffix\n            for alpha in ALPHAS[1:]:\n                path = os.path.join(results_dir,\n                    \"%s_H96_K5_dense_frozen_%d_router-partial_alpha-%.2f.json\" % (ds, seed, alpha))\n                if os.path.exists(path):\n                    with open(path) as f:\n                        data = json.load(f)\n                    dose[ds][alpha].append(data[\"rr_moa\"][\"mse\"])\n                    entropy[ds][alpha].append(data[\"rr_moa\"][\"routing_entropy\"])\n\n    # Shuffle results\n    shuffle = defaultdict(list)  # shuffle[dataset] = [(mse, entropy), ...]\n    for ds in DATASETS:\n        for seed in SEEDS:\n            path = os.path.join(results_dir,\n                \"%s_H96_K5_dense_frozen_%d_router-shuffled.json\" % (ds, seed))\n            if os.path.exists(path):\n                with open(path) as f:\n                    data = json.load(f)\n                shuffle[ds].append({\n                    \"mse\": data[\"rr_moa\"][\"mse\"],\n                    \"entropy\": data[\"rr_moa\"][\"routing_entropy\"],\n                })\n\n    return dose, entropy, shuffle\n\n\ndef check_monotonicity(values):\n    \"\"\"Check if a sequence is monotonically non-decreasing.\"\"\"\n    for i in range(1, len(values)):\n        if values[i] < values[i-1] - 1e-6:\n            return False\n    return True\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    parser.add_argument(\"--latex\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Emit LaTeX table\")\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    dose, entropy, shuffle = load_results()\n\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"NORMALIZATION DOSE-RESPONSE ANALYSIS\")\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n\n    # Per-dataset table\n    all_monotone_mse = 0\n    all_monotone_ent = 0\n    total_ds = 0\n\n    for ds in DATASETS:\n        if not dose[ds]:\n            print(\"\\n%s: NO DATA\" % ds)\n            continue\n\n        total_ds += 1\n        print(\"\\n%s:\" % ds)\n        print(\"  %-8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %3s  %3s\" % (\n            \"alpha\", \"MSE_mean\", \"MSE_std\", \"Ent_mean\", \"Ent_std\", \"n_m\", \"n_e\"))\n\n        mse_means = []\n        ent_means = []\n        for alpha in ALPHAS:\n            mse_vals = dose[ds].get(alpha, [])\n            ent_vals = entropy[ds].get(alpha, [])\n            if mse_vals:\n                m_mean, m_std = np.mean(mse_vals), np.std(mse_vals)\n                e_mean, e_std = np.mean(ent_vals), np.std(ent_vals)\n                mse_means.append(m_mean)\n                ent_means.append(e_mean)\n                print(\"  %-8.2f  %8.4f  %8.4f  %8.3f  %8.3f  %3d  %3d\" % (\n                    alpha, m_mean, m_std, e_mean, e_std, len(mse_vals), len(ent_vals)))\n            else:\n                print(\"  %-8.2f  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %3d  %3d\" % (\n                    alpha, \"---\", \"---\", \"---\", \"---\", 0, 0))\n\n        if len(mse_means) >= 3:\n            mono_mse = check_monotonicity(mse_means)\n            mono_ent = check_monotonicity([-e for e in ent_means])  # entropy should decrease\n            all_monotone_mse += int(mono_mse)\n            all_monotone_ent += int(mono_ent)\n            print(\"  Monotone MSE (increasing): %s\" % (\"YES\" if mono_mse else \"NO\"))\n            print(\"  Monotone entropy (decreasing): %s\" % (\"YES\" if mono_ent else \"NO\"))\n\n            if mse_means[0] > 0 and mse_means[-1] > 0:\n                degradation = (mse_means[-1] - mse_means[0]) / mse_means[0] * 100\n                print(\"  MSE degradation (alpha=0 -> 1): +%.1f%%\" % degradation)\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"MONOTONICITY SUMMARY: MSE %d/%d, Entropy %d/%d\" % (\n        all_monotone_mse, total_ds, all_monotone_ent, total_ds))\n\n    # Shuffle analysis\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"SHUFFLE ABLATION\")\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"  %-12s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %3s\" % (\n        \"Dataset\", \"Raw_MSE\", \"Shuf_MSE\", \"Shuf_Ent\", \"Rev_MSE\", \"Delta%\", \"n\"))\n\n    for ds in DATASETS:\n        raw_mse = dose[ds].get(0.0, [])\n        rev_mse = dose[ds].get(1.0, [])\n        shuf_data = shuffle.get(ds, [])\n\n        if raw_mse and shuf_data:\n            raw_m = np.mean(raw_mse)\n            shuf_m = np.mean([s[\"mse\"] for s in shuf_data])\n            shuf_e = np.mean([s[\"entropy\"] for s in shuf_data])\n            rev_m = np.mean(rev_mse) if rev_mse else float('nan')\n            delta = (shuf_m - raw_m) / raw_m * 100\n            print(\"  %-12s  %8.4f  %8.4f  %8.3f  %8.4f  %+7.1f%%  %3d\" % (\n                ds, raw_m, shuf_m, shuf_e, rev_m, delta, len(shuf_data)))\n        else:\n            print(\"  %-12s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %8s  %3d\" % (\n                ds, \"---\", \"---\", \"---\", \"---\", \"---\", len(shuf_data)))\n\n    # Cross-dataset summary for paper\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 80)\n    print(\"PAPER-READY SUMMARY\")\n    print(\"=\" * 80)\n\n    # Compute mean degradation per alpha step\n    all_degradations = []\n    for ds in DATASETS:\n        mse_means = []\n        for alpha in ALPHAS:\n            vals = dose[ds].get(alpha, [])\n            if vals:\n                mse_means.append(np.mean(vals))\n        if len(mse_means) == len(ALPHAS):\n            for i in range(1, len(ALPHAS)):\n                step_deg = (mse_means[i] - mse_means[0]) / mse_means[0] * 100\n                all_degradations.append((ALPHAS[i], ds, step_deg))\n\n    if all_degradations:\n        print(\"\\nMean MSE degradation vs alpha=0 (across datasets):\")\n        for alpha in ALPHAS[1:]:\n            degs = [d for a, _, d in all_degradations if a == alpha]\n            if degs:\n                print(\"  alpha=%.2f: +%.1f%% mean (+%.1f%% to +%.1f%%)\" % (\n                    alpha, np.mean(degs), np.min(degs), np.max(degs)))\n\n    if args.latex:\n        print(\"\\n% LaTeX table (dose-response)\")\n        print(\"\\\\begin{tabular}{l\" + \"cc\" * len(ALPHAS) + \"}\")\n        print(\"\\\\toprule\")\n        header = \"Dataset\"\n        for a in ALPHAS:\n            header += \" & MSE & Ent\"\n        print(header + \" \\\\\\\\\")\n        alpha_header = \"\"\n        for a in ALPHAS:\n            alpha_header += \" & $\\\\alpha=%.2f$\" % a + \" &\"\n        print(\"\\\\midrule\")\n\n        for ds in DATASETS:\n            row = ds\n            for alpha in ALPHAS:\n                mse_vals = dose[ds].get(alpha, [])\n                ent_vals = entropy[ds].get(alpha, [])\n                if mse_vals:\n                    row += \" & %.3f & %.2f\" % (np.mean(mse_vals), np.mean(ent_vals))\n                else:\n                    row += \" & --- & ---\"\n            print(row + \" \\\\\\\\\")\n\n        print(\"\\\\bottomrule\")\n        print(\"\\\\end{tabular}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}
