```
74	\textbf{(2)~Seven converging causal controls via opposite-trajectory analysis.} The strongest evidence: under identical unfreezing on identical data, MOMENT entropy collapses ($0.60{\to}0.00$) while Timer-XL (no RevIN) entropy \emph{rises} ($1.49{\to}1.60$; Figure~\ref{fig:causal_contrast})---the same code, the same data, only the normalization differs. Six additional controls---RevIN ablation, Moirai/Chronos negative controls, RevIN-applied-to-router, entropy regularization, and a 720-run rescue sweep~\citep{fedus2022switch, zoph2022stmoe, zhang2025remoe, zhou2022expertchoice} (Table~\ref{tab:rescue})---all converge on the same diagnosis.
75	
76	\textbf{(3)~RR-MoA and the Frozen Paradox.} RR-MoA achieves \textbf{54/54 wins} ($p{<}0.001$, Wilcoxon; Table~\ref{tab:baselines}), outperforming LoRA, TRACE, ensembles, and full fine-tuning. Frozen RR-MoA beats full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\%$---the \textbf{Frozen Paradox}. RR-MoA generalizes across four backbones with instance normalization (MOMENT-small/large, Moirai, Moirai-MoE); two negative controls without it (Chronos, Timer-XL) confirm normalization-specificity. Dual-stream experts close the DLinear gap from $+57\%$ to $+5\%$, \textbf{beating} DLinear on Weather ($0.198$ vs $0.208$, $p{=}0.03$; \S\ref{sec:main_results}).
77	
78	\section{Related Work}
79	
80	\textbf{TSFM Adaptation.} Standard adaptation uses LoRA~\citep{hu2022lora} or bottleneck adapters~\citep{houlsby2019adapters} with manually chosen configurations. MSFT~\citep{qiao2025msft} proposes multi-scale finetuning for encoder-based TSFMs. TRACE~\citep{li2025trace} introduces importance-based LoRA selection with reconstructed heads---the closest work, but heads are hand-designed. Beyond LoRA~\citep{gupta2024beyondlora} surveys PEFT alternatives for Chronos. AdaPTS~\citep{benechehab2025adapts} adapts input representations. Prune-then-Finetune~\citep{zhao2025prune} applies structured pruning. In-context fine-tuning~\citep{faw2025icf} adapts at inference time. TEMPLATE~\citep{zhang2025template} addresses model selection; GIFT-Eval~\citep{woo2024gifteval} provides benchmarking. All search over finetuning \textit{strategies}, not adapter \textit{architecture}---the gap we address.
81	
82	\textbf{MoE in Foundation Models.} Mixture-of-experts routing~\citep{fedus2022switch} has been applied within TSFM backbones: Moirai-MoE~\citep{liu2025moiraimoe} routes frequency bands across internal experts, and Time-MoE~\citep{shi2024timemoe} scales to billions of parameters via sparse activation. These apply MoE \emph{within} the backbone architecture; segment-wise approaches further specialize experts by temporal segments. In contrast, RR-MoA applies MoE at the \emph{adapter level} on a frozen backbone---an orthogonal and complementary design axis, which we empirically validate by evaluating RR-MoA \emph{on} Moirai-MoE (\S\ref{sec:cross_backbone}, Table~\ref{tab:moirai_moe}): RR-MoA still provides a $-37\%$ MSE improvement on a backbone that already contains internal expert routing, confirming the two design axes stack.
83	
84	\textbf{MoE routing collapse.} A large body of work addresses routing collapse via \emph{optimization-side} interventions: Switch Transformer load-balance~\citep{fedus2022switch}, ST-MoE z-loss~\citep{zoph2022stmoe}, Expert-Choice routing~\citep{zhou2022expertchoice}, and ReMoE ReLU gates~\citep{zhang2025remoe}. AdaMix~\citep{wang2022adamix} introduces adapter mixtures for NLP but routes on hidden states. All enforce diversity via optimization constraints without addressing whether the router's input contains discriminative information. We sweep eleven such mechanisms on MOMENT$+$RevIN and show \textbf{none recover MSE to within $2.7{\times}$ of RR-MoA} (Table~\ref{tab:rescue}). Our contribution is complementary: we identify when routing information is structurally absent from hidden states, and propose moving the router's input out of the normalized representation as the minimal causal fix.
85	
86	\textbf{Normalization in Time Series.} RevIN~\citep{kim2021revin} mitigates distribution shift; DishTS~\citep{fan2023dishts} and Non-stationary Transformers~\citep{liu2022nonstationary} partially recover stripped statistics---both recognizing that instance normalization discards predictive information. We identify a previously unknown consequence: RevIN destroys the per-window statistics that MoE routers require for discriminative gating. RR-MoA operationalizes the ``statistic re-injection'' insight specifically for routing.
87	
88	\textbf{NAS and LLM-Guided Search.} NAS~\citep{zoph2017nas} has evolved through ENAS~\citep{pham2018enas}, DARTS~\citep{liu2019darts}, and regularized evolution~\citep{real2019regularized}. Shears~\citep{munoz2024shears} and RZ-NAS~\citep{ji2025rznas} search adapter hyperparameters for LLMs; NAS-LoRA~\citep{chen2025naslora} targets vision adapters. FunSearch~\citep{romera2024funsearch}, EvoPrompting~\citep{chen2023evoprompting}, PartEvo~\citep{hu2025partevo}, and ReEvo~\citep{ye2024reevo} demonstrate LLM-guided program synthesis. We use this paradigm for expert pool construction (Appendix~\ref{app:aas_details}).
```

```
413	
414	Bypass alone is insufficient: entropy remains high ($1.50$--$1.57$) but MSE degrades $60$--$88\%$, confirming the router relies on the \emph{content} of the stripped statistics.
415	
416	\textbf{Normalization dose-response.} Interpolating the router input $\mathbf{x}_\alpha = (1{-}\alpha)\,\mathbf{x}_\text{raw} + \alpha\,\text{RevIN}(\mathbf{x}_\text{raw})$ reveals a \emph{phase transition}, not a binary switch (Table~\ref{tab:dose_response}; 5 seeds $\times$ 6 datasets = 150 runs). MSE degrades mildly for $\alpha \in [0, 0.75]$ (mean $+5.4\%$) but catastrophically at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (mean $+283\%$; $+81\%$ to $+761\%$). Crucially, entropy \emph{does not collapse}---it stays in $[1.43, 1.57]$ across all $\alpha$. The router makes diversified but uninformative decisions under normalization: a more nuanced failure than pure entropy collapse. An orthogonal \textbf{temporal-shuffle ablation} (routing on time-permuted raw input) performs within ${\pm}3\%$ of raw on 4/6 datasets, confirming routing depends on distributional statistics $(M,\Sigma)$ rather than temporal ordering---consistent with Proposition~\ref{thm:mi_decomp} (Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}).

438	
439	\textbf{Why RevIN destroys routing: formal analysis.} Each input window $\mathbf{x}_i$ decomposes into mean $M_i = \mu(\mathbf{x}_i)$, scale $\Sigma_i = \sigma(\mathbf{x}_i)$, and shape $\mathbf{S}_i = (\mathbf{x}_i - M_i)/\Sigma_i$. RevIN maps $\mathbf{x}_i \mapsto \mathbf{S}_i$, discarding $(M_i, \Sigma_i)$.
440	
441	\begin{proposition}[Routing Information Loss Under RevIN]
442	\label{thm:mi_decomp}
443	Let $X$ be a random input window, $E \in \{1,\ldots,K\}$ the expert assignment produced by any deterministic router, and $S = (X - M)/\Sigma$ the RevIN-normalized shape.
444	\begin{enumerate}
445	    \item[\textup{(i)}] \textbf{Exact decomposition.} $I(X; E) - I(S; E) = I(M, \Sigma; E \mid S) \geq 0$.
446	    \item[\textup{(ii)}] \textbf{Unconditional quantitative bound.} Without any independence assumption,
447	    \[
448	        I(X; E) - I(S; E) \;\geq\; I(M, \Sigma;\, E) \;-\; I(M, \Sigma;\, S),
449	    \]
450	    where the residual $\varepsilon := I(M, \Sigma;\, S) \geq 0$ measures the ``leakage'' of stripped statistics through the shape.  Equality (tightness) holds iff $(M,\Sigma) \perp\!\!\!\perp S$ given $E$.
451	    \item[\textup{(iii)}] \textbf{Full-information corner.} If the router depends \emph{entirely} on the stripped statistics ($E \perp\!\!\!\perp S \mid (M,\Sigma)$), then $I(X; E) - I(S; E) = H(E)$: RevIN destroys the \emph{entire} routing distribution in nats.
452	    \item[\textup{(iv)}] \textbf{Empirical leakage and signal ratio.} The leakage $\varepsilon$ varies by dataset ($0.09$--$1.26$ nats via CCA; cross-validated within ${\pm}0.2$ nats by the distribution-free KSG estimator~\citep{kraskov2004ksg}). The bound~(ii) is informative when $I(M,\Sigma; E) \gg \varepsilon$. The signal ratio $R(\mathcal{D}) = [\mathrm{Var}(M) + \mathrm{Var}(\Sigma)] / \overline{\mathrm{Var}(S)}$ serves as a monotone proxy for $I(M,\Sigma; E)$: high-$R$ datasets have large routing information relative to $\varepsilon$, producing large raw-routing gains; low-$R$ datasets have $I(M,\Sigma; E) \approx \varepsilon$, producing no gain---validated at $\rho{=}{-}0.88$ across nine datasets.
453	\end{enumerate}
454	\end{proposition}
455	
456	Full proof in Appendix~\ref{app:proofs}. \textit{Remark.} $R$ is monotone in $I(M,\Sigma; E)$: higher location-scale variance gives the softmax router more discriminative power; dividing by $\overline{\mathrm{Var}(S)}$ accounts for leakage $\varepsilon$. KSG cross-check (${\pm}0.26$ nats) confirms this holds beyond Gaussianity.
457	
458	\textbf{Quantitative prediction.} The \emph{routing signal ratio} $R(\mathcal{D}) = [\text{Var}(M) + \text{Var}(\Sigma)] / \overline{\text{Var}(S)}$ serves as a monotone proxy for the MI bound. Figure~\ref{fig:signal_ratio} validates this across nine LTSF benchmarks: $\rho = -0.88$ ($p < 0.002$; bootstrap CI: $[-1.00, -0.51]$; $n{=}9$). Traffic ($R{=}0.14$) is correctly predicted as a boundary case ($\Delta{=}{+}2.9\%$). Solar ($R{=}0.06$) is the one outlier: the Conv1d router exploits temporal shape patterns beyond location-scale, consistent with the SSR ablation ($+43\%$ degradation). Additional ablations (SSR, RDGF, per-quartile analysis) in Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}.
459	
460	\begin{figure}[t]
461	\centering
462	\begin{minipage}[t]{0.58\columnwidth}
```

```
\textbf{Normalization dose-response.} Interpolating the router input $\mathbf{x}_\alpha = (1{-}\alpha)\,\mathbf{x}_\text{raw} + \alpha\,\text{RevIN}(\mathbf{x}_\text{raw})$ reveals a \emph{phase transition}, not a binary switch (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}; 5 seeds $\times$ 6 datasets = 150 runs). MSE degrades mildly for $\alpha \in [0, 0.75]$ (mean $+5.4\%$) but catastrophically at $\alpha{=}1.0$ (mean $+283\%$; $+81\%$ to $+761\%$). Crucially, entropy \emph{does not collapse}---it stays in $[1.43, 1.57]$ across all $\alpha$. Normalization does not collapse routing---it renders routing uninformative: the router makes diversified but wrong decisions. An orthogonal \textbf{temporal-shuffle ablation} (routing on time-permuted raw input) performs within ${\pm}3\%$ of raw on 4/6 datasets, confirming routing depends on distributional statistics $(M,\Sigma)$ rather than temporal ordering---consistent with Proposition~\ref{thm:mi_decomp} (Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}).
\label{fig:dose_response}
```

```json
{
```

```json
  "file_path": "/Users/hungpq2412/neuralips26/main.tex",
  "old_string": "\\begin{abstract}\nStandard mixture-of-experts (MoE) routing on time series foundation models (TSFMs) collapses catastrophically: routing entropy drops to $0.000{\\pm}0.000$ under fine-tuning on backbones with Reversible Instance Normalization (RevIN). We diagnose the cause as \\textbf{normalization-induced co-adaptation}---RevIN strips per-window mean and scale that the router needs for discriminative gating---and prove it via seven converging controls, including a 720-run rescue sweep where all eleven standard mechanisms fail ($2.7{\\times}$ worse than our fix) and opposite entropy trajectories on Timer-XL (no RevIN): entropy rises from $1.49{\\to}1.60$ under unfreezing, vs $0.60{\\to}0.00$ on MOMENT. A mutual-information decomposition (Proposition~\\ref{thm:mi_decomp}) predicts which datasets benefit, validated at Spearman $\\rho{=}{-}0.88$ ($p{<}0.002$, $n{=}9$)---including correctly predicting where raw routing does \\emph{not} help (Traffic, $R{=}0.14$). To break the collapse, we propose \\textbf{Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters (RR-MoA)}: a lightweight gate routes on the raw pre-normalization input while a frozen backbone produces embeddings. RR-MoA achieves \\textbf{54/54 wins} ($p{<}0.001$, Wilcoxon) on the primary backbone and generalizes across four additional backbones with instance normalization; two negative controls without it confirm normalization-specificity. The \\textbf{Frozen Paradox}: frozen RR-MoA outperforms full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\\%$. A second diagnostic sweep uncovers gate-initialization pathology; its fix, \\textbf{Residual-IA\\textsuperscript{+}}, achieves \\textbf{parity-or-better with DLinear on $108/123$ cells} across six backbones, six datasets, and four horizons (Timer-XL: $12/12$; Moirai-MoE: $15/15$; $65$ significant wins vs $11$ losses).\n\\end{abstract}",
  "new_string": "\\begin{abstract}\nWe discover a fundamental incompatibility between instance normalization and mixture-of-experts (MoE) routing on time series foundation models (TSFMs): routing entropy drops to $0.000{\\pm}0.000$, yet the failure is not entropy collapse---a normalization dose-response reveals that entropy stays high while MSE degrades $283\\%$ (phase transition at full normalization). The cause is \\textbf{normalization-induced co-adaptation}: RevIN strips per-window mean and scale that the router needs for discriminative gating. Seven converging controls isolate this, including opposite entropy trajectories on Timer-XL (no RevIN, entropy \\emph{rises} $1.49{\\to}1.60$) and a 720-run rescue sweep where all eleven standard mechanisms fail ($2.7{\\times}$ worse). A mutual-information decomposition (Proposition~\\ref{thm:mi_decomp}) predicts which datasets benefit ($\\rho{=}{-}0.88$, $p{<}0.002$, $n{=}9$), including where it does \\emph{not} help (Traffic, $R{=}0.14$). The diagnosis is precise enough that the fix is unambiguous: \\textbf{Raw-Routed Mixture of Adapters (RR-MoA)} routes on raw pre-normalization input while the backbone stays frozen. RR-MoA achieves \\textbf{54/54 wins} ($p{<}0.001$) on the primary backbone, generalizes across four additional backbones (two negative controls confirm normalization-specificity), and produces the \\textbf{Frozen Paradox}: frozen beats full fine-tuning by $12$--$79\\%$. \\textbf{Residual-IA\\textsuperscript{+}} achieves \\textbf{parity-or-better with DLinear on $108/123$ cells} across six backbones (Timer-XL $12/12$; Moirai-MoE $15/15$; $65$ significant wins vs $11$ losses).\n\\end{abstract}"
}
```

```
416:{\looseness=-1 \textbf{Normalization dose-response.} Interpolating $\mathbf{x}_\alpha = (1{-}\alpha)\,\mathbf{x}_\text{raw} + \alpha\,\text{RevIN}(\mathbf{x}_\text{raw})$ reveals a \emph{phase transition} (Figure~\ref{fig:dose_response}; 150 runs). MSE degrades mildly for $\alpha{\leq}0.75$ ($+5.4\%$) but catastrophically at $\alpha{=}1.0$ ($+283\%$). Entropy \emph{does not collapse}---it stays in $[1.43, 1.57]$ at all $\alpha$: normalization renders routing uninformative without reducing diversity. A \textbf{temporal-shuffle ablation} (time-permuted raw input) performs within ${\pm}3\%$ of raw on $4/6$ datasets, confirming routing depends on $(M,\Sigma)$ statistics, not temporal ordering (Appendix~\ref{app:routing_ablations}).}
475:\label{fig:dose_response}
```
